&lt;project_context>
# 项目: trace-verdict-study

## 运行环境
- 服务器名: westd-11235
- SSH 账户: root（调用 ssh_execute / submit_training_job 时传入 account="root"）
- SSH 连接命令: ssh connect.westd.seetacloud.com  # 必须用此命令，~/.ssh/config 自动应用密钥和跳板机，禁止用裸 IP
- 项目代码根目录: /root/trace-verdict-study


## 环境搭建
连接服务器后先调 `detect_image` 检测是否为预置镜像。如果匹配，跳过基础环境搭建，只装项目特有依赖。已预装的包不要重复安装。

## 输出风格
回复简洁直接。只输出执行结果和必要信息，不要解释过程、不要客套、不要给未被要求的建议。
&lt;/project_context>
**任务**: 修复论文数据错误 + 生成 tokenizer 统计存档。

**服务器**: westd-11235 (root@westd-11235)

## Part 1: 必修 — 修复 experiments.tex L38

将 experiments.tex 第 38 行（或附近）的 "2,759" 改为 "2,626"。

当前错误：用了 full_trace 的 DeepSeek mean token count (2759)，应该是 VOTA 的 mean (2626)。截断率 91.7% 不变（这个数字是对的）。

```bash
# 找到具体位置
grep -n "2.759\|2,759" /root/trace-verdict-study/paper/experiments.tex
# 替换
sed -i 's/2,759/2,626/g' /root/trace-verdict-study/paper/experiments.tex
# 验证
grep -n "2.626\|2,626" /root/trace-verdict-study/paper/experiments.tex
```

## Part 2: 生成完整 tokenizer 统计

写一个 Python 脚本 `/root/trace-verdict-study/analysis/tokenizer_stats.py`，计算 Qwen 和 DeepSeek tokenizer 对每个 condition 的 mean token count。

数据文件位置：`/root/autodl-tmp/trace-verdict-study-data/`

需要统计的 conditions（每个都有 _train.jsonl 和 _test.jsonl）：
- vota
- no_trace
- exception_only
- full_trace
- ae_only
- loop_only

对每个 condition 的每个样本：
1. 用 Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct 的 tokenizer 编码（包含 chat template）
2. 用 DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct 的 tokenizer 编码（包含 chat template）
3. 统计 mean, median, max, >2048 比例

输出：
- 打印统计表到 stdout
- 保存 CSV 到 `/root/trace-verdict-study/analysis/tokenizer_stats.csv`
- 保存 JSON 到 `/root/trace-verdict-study/analysis/tokenizer_stats.json`

**注意**: 
- 模型路径在 `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/` 下
- 如果加载两个 tokenizer 显存不够（GPU 正在训练），只加载 tokenizer（不加载模型权重），tokenizer 不需要 GPU
- 环境: `source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base`
- 验证 VOTA 的 DeepSeek mean 确实是 2626（不是 2759），确认论文修正正确

## Part 3: 编译验证

```bash
cd /root/trace-verdict-study/paper && pdflatex -interaction=nonstopmode main.tex 2>&1 | grep -E "^!|Error" | head -5
```

**产出**: 修复确认 + tokenizer_stats.csv/json 路径 + 关键统计数字

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\\section{Experiments}\\n\\nWe evaluate our trace preprocessing pipeline across two research questions:\\n(1)~Do Tier~1 exception signals generalize across models of different capability?\\n(2)~What is the relative contribution of each trace component to verdict prediction accuracy?\\nAll experiments use our trace--verdict dataset (\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:dataset}) with $N{=}1{,}190$ test samples; accuracy is reported with Wilson 95\\\\% confidence intervals unless stated otherwise.\\nPairwise comparisons use paired bootstrap resampling ($B{=}10{,}000$, seed=42); all cross-condition differences reported below are significant at $p{<}0.0001$ unless noted otherwise.\\n\\n\\\\subsection{Cross-Model Comparison}\\n\\\\label{sec:cross_model}\\n\\nTo test whether exception-based trace compression transfers across model families, we compare Qwen-2.5-7B-Instruct and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct under four input conditions: \\\\texttt{exception\\\\_only} (Tier~1 exception events), VOTA (exception + assertion-point + loop compression), \\\\texttt{full\\\\_trace} (complete execution trace), and \\\\texttt{no\\\\_trace} (source code only).\\n\\n\\\\begin{table}[t]\\n\\\\centering\\n\\\\small\\n\\\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\\n\\\\toprule\\n\\\\textbf{Condition} & \\\\textbf{Qwen-2.5-7B} & \\\\textbf{DeepSeek-6.7B} & \\\\textbf{Gap} \\\\\\\\\\n\\\\midrule\\nexception\\\\_only & \\\\textbf{99.83}\\\\% & 99.33\\\\%$^\\\\dag$ & 0.50pp \\\\\\\\\\nVOTA & 99.41\\\\% & 84.45\\\\% & 14.96pp \\\\\\\\\\nVOTA\\\\_no\\\\_ae & 99.41\\\\%$^\\\\S$ & --- & --- \\\\\\\\\\nfull\\\\_trace & 81.76\\\\% & N/A$^\\\\ddag$ & --- \\\\\\\\\\nno\\\\_trace & 75.13\\\\% & 73.36\\\\% & 1.77pp \\\\\\\\\\n\\\\midrule\\nMajority baseline & 71.51\\\\% & 71.51\\\\% & --- \\\\\\\\\\n\\\\bottomrule\\n\\\\end{tabular}\\n\\\\caption{Cross-model accuracy ($N{=}1{,}190$). Qwen CIs: exception\\\\_only [99.58, 100.00], VOTA [98.82, 99.83], full\\\\_trace [79.70, 83.90], no\\\\_trace [72.77, 77.48]; DeepSeek CIs: VOTA [82.60, 86.40], no\\\\_trace [70.80, 75.70]. $^\\\\dag$DeepSeek \\\\texttt{exception\\\\_only} 3-seed results: 99.33\\\\% (seeds 42, 456; CI [98.82, 99.75]) and 91.09\\\\% (seed 123; CI [89.40, 92.60]); seed 123 is a LoRA initialization outlier (fail recall = 70.5\\\\% vs.\\\\ 100\\\\%). $^\\\\S$VOTA\\\\_no\\\\_ae evaluated on Qwen only; CI [98.90, 99.80]. $^\\\\ddag$DeepSeek full\\\\_trace was invalidated because the DeepSeek tokenizer produced sequences exceeding 2{,}048 tokens, causing the verdict label to be truncated during training; the model consequently failed to learn the classification task.}\\n\\\\label{tab:cross_model}\\n\\\\end{table}\\n\\nTable~\\\\ref{tab:cross_model} reveals a striking asymmetry.\\nUnder \\\\texttt{exception\\\\_only}, Qwen achieves 99.83\\\\% $\\\\pm$ 0.00 across three seeds, while DeepSeek shows a bimodal pattern: seeds 42 and 456 both achieve 99.33\\\\% (gap = 0.50pp), while seed 123 yields 91.09\\\\% (gap = 8.74pp), attributable to suboptimal LoRA initialization (fail recall = 70.5\\\\% vs.\\\\ 100\\\\% for other seeds).\\nEven the outlier seed outperforms VOTA (84.45\\\\%) by 6.64pp, confirming exception events as the most effective component across models.\\nUnder VOTA, the gap widens to 14.96pp: DeepSeek drops to 84.45\\\\% while Qwen remains at 99.41\\\\%.\\nPost-hoc tokenizer analysis reveals a confound: measured on the training set using each model's tokenizer, the DeepSeek tokenizer averages 2{,}759 tokens per VOTA sample versus 1{,}908 for Qwen, causing 91.7\\\\% of DeepSeek VOTA training samples to exceed the 2{,}048-token context window.\\nUnlike the DeepSeek \\\\texttt{full\\\\_trace} condition (Table~\\\\ref{tab:cross_model}, $^\\\\ddag$), where right-truncation removed the verdict label entirely, the VOTA training framework preserves the verdict label but truncates the trace content (input), so the model trains with incomplete diagnostic information for the vast majority of the dataset.\\nThe 14.96pp gap thus reflects both model capability differences and training-time trace truncation; the two factors cannot be disentangled in the current design.\\nHowever, a post-hoc group analysis provides directional evidence: partitioning DeepSeek VOTA test samples by whether their training-set counterparts exceeded the context window, intact samples ($N{=}99$) achieve 100\\\\% accuracy while truncated samples ($N{=}1{,}091$) achieve 83.04\\\\% (95\\\\% CI [80.75\\\\%, 85.24\\\\%]; Fisher exact $p{<}0.001$), with all 185 errors concentrated in the fail class of the truncated group (fail recall $=$ 31.23\\\\%).\\nIn contrast, the \\\\texttt{exception\\\\_only} condition is unaffected by truncation because exception-based inputs are short enough to fit within the context window for both tokenizers, making its cross-model comparison clean.\\nPer-class analysis (Appendix~\\\\ref{sec:per_class}) reveals the mechanism: DeepSeek VOTA achieves 100\\\\% fail precision but only 45.43\\\\% fail recall, systematically defaulting to \\\\texttt{pass}---in contrast to Qwen VOTA, which achieves 100\\\\% fail recall.\\n\\nThe \\\\texttt{no\\\\_trace} baseline confirms that source code alone provides limited signal (75.13\\\\% for Qwen, 73.36\\\\% for DeepSeek), only marginally above majority-class guessing (71.51\\\\%).\\n\\nSeed robustness diverges sharply across models.\\nQwen \\\\texttt{exception\\\\_only} produces identical accuracy of 99.83\\\\% across all three seeds ($\\\\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$, exactly two errors each).\\nDeepSeek shows a bimodal pattern ($n{=}3$): seeds 42 and 456 both achieve 99.33\\\\% (8 errors each, fail recall = 100\\\\%), while seed 123 yields 91.09\\\\% (fail recall = 70.5\\\\%), consistent with LoRA initialization sensitivity on the smaller model.\\nBecause the \\\\texttt{exception\\\\_only} test set contains 48.99\\\\% duplicate inputs (607 unique out of 1,190; \\\\S\\\\ref{sec:dataset}), the reported CIs assume $n{=}1{,}190$ independent trials.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

the model consequently failed to learn the classification task.}\\n\\\\label{tab:cross_model}\\n\\\\end{table}\\n\\nTable~\\\\ref{tab:cross_model} reveals a striking asymmetry.\\nUnder \\\\texttt{exception\\\\_only}, Qwen achieves 99.83\\\\% $\\\\pm$ 0.00 across three seeds, while DeepSeek shows a bimodal pattern: seeds 42 and 456 both achieve 99.33\\\\% (gap = 0.50pp), while seed 123 yields 91.09\\\\% (gap = 8.74pp), attributable to suboptimal LoRA initialization (fail recall = 70.5\\\\% vs.\\\\ 100\\\\% for other seeds).\\nEven the outlier seed outperforms VOTA (84.45\\\\%) by 6.64pp, confirming exception events as the most effective component across models.\\nUnder VOTA, the gap widens to 14.96pp: DeepSeek drops to 84.45\\\\% while Qwen remains at 99.41\\\\%.\\nPost-hoc tokenizer analysis reveals a confound: measured on the training set using each model's tokenizer, the DeepSeek tokenizer averages 2{,}759 tokens per VOTA sample versus 1{,}908 for Qwen, causing 91.7\\\\% of DeepSeek VOTA training samples to exceed the 2{,}048-token context window.\\nUnlike the DeepSeek \\\\texttt{full\\\\_trace} condition (Table~\\\\ref{tab:cross_model}, $^\\\\ddag$), where right-truncation removed the verdict label entirely, the VOTA training framework preserves the verdict label but truncates the trace content (input), so the model trains with incomplete diagnostic information for the vast majority of the dataset.\\nThe 14.96pp gap thus reflects both model capability differences and training-time trace truncation; the two factors cannot be disentangled in the current design.\\nHowever, a post-hoc group analysis provides directional evidence: partitioning DeepSeek VOTA test samples by whether their training-set counterparts exceeded the context window, intact samples ($N{=}99$) achieve 100\\\\% accuracy while truncated samples ($N{=}1{,}091$) achieve 83.04\\\\% (95\\\\% CI [80.75\\\\%, 85.24\\\\%]; Fisher exact $p{<}0.001$), with all 185 errors concentrated in the fail class of the truncated group (fail recall $=$ 31.23\\\\%).\\nIn contrast, the \\\\texttt{exception\\\\_only} condition is unaffected by truncation because exception-based inputs are short enough to fit within the context window for both tokenizers, making its cross-model comparison clean.\\nPer-class analysis (Appendix~\\\\ref{sec:per_class}) reveals the mechanism: DeepSeek VOTA achieves 100\\\\% fail precision but only 45.43\\\\% fail recall, systematically defaulting to \\\\texttt{pass}---in contrast to Qwen VOTA, which achieves 100\\\\% fail recall.\\n\\nThe \\\\texttt{no\\\\_trace} baseline confirms that source code alone provides limited signal (75.13\\\\% for Qwen, 73.36\\\\% for DeepSeek), only marginally above majority-class guessing (71.51\\\\%).\\n\\nSeed robustness diverges sharply across models.\\nQwen \\\\texttt{exception\\\\_only} produces identical accuracy of 99.83\\\\% across all three seeds ($\\\\sigma{=}0$, $n{=}3$, exactly two errors each).\\nDeepSeek shows a bimodal pattern ($n{=}3$): seeds 42 and 456 both achieve 99.33\\\\% (8 errors each, fail recall = 100\\\\%), while seed 123 yields 91.09\\\\% (fail recall = 70.5\\\\%), consistent with LoRA initialization sensitivity on the smaller model.\\nBecause the \\\\texttt{exception\\\\_only} test set contains 48.99\\\\% duplicate inputs (607 unique out of 1,190; \\\\S\\\\ref{sec:dataset}), the reported CIs assume $n{=}1{,}190$ independent trials.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"

{
  "server": "westd-11235",
  "command": "cat > /root/trace-verdict-study/analysis/tokenizer_stats.py << 'PYEOF'\nimport json\nimport csv\nimport os\nimport numpy as np\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer\n\nDATA_DIR = Path(\"/root/autodl-tmp/trace-verdict-study-data\")\nOUT_DIR = Path(\"/root/trace-verdict-study/analysis\")\nHF_CACHE = \"/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache\"\n\nCONDITIONS = [\"vota\", \"no_trace\", \"exception_only\", \"full_trace\", \"ae_only\", \"loop_only\"]\nSPLITS = [\"train\", \"test\"]\n\nMODELS = {\n    \"Qwen2.5-Coder-7B\": \"Qwen/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct\",\n    \"DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B\": \"deepseek-ai/deepseek-coder-6.7b-instruct\",\n}\n\ndef load_samples(condition, split):\n    path = DATA_DIR / f\"{condition}_{split}.jsonl\"\n    samples = []\n    with open(path) as f:\n        for line in f:\n            obj = json.loads(line)\n            samples.append(obj)\n    return samples\n\ndef tokenize_with_chat_template(tokenizer, sample):\n    messages = [\n        {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": sample[\"input\"]},\n        {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": sample[\"output\"]},\n    ]\n    text = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(messages, tokenize=False)\n    tokens = tokenizer.encode(text)\n    return len(tokens)\n\ndef compute_stats(token_counts, ctx_limit=2048):\n    arr = np.array(token_counts)\n    return {\n        \"mean\": float(np.mean(arr)),\n        \"median\": float(np.median(arr)),\n        \"max\": int(np.max(arr)),\n        \"pct_over_2048\": float(np.mean(arr > ctx_limit) * 100),\n        \"n\": len(arr),\n    }\n\ndef main():\n    print(\"Loading tokenizers (CPU only)...\")\n    tokenizers = {}\n    for name, model_id in MODELS.items():\n        print(f\"  Loading {name}...\")\n        tokenizers[name] = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(\n            model_id, cache_dir=HF_CACHE, trust_remote_code=True\n        )\n    \n    all_results = []\n    \n    for condition in CONDITIONS:\n        for split in SPLITS:\n            samples = load_samples(condition, split)\n            for model_name, tok in tokenizers.items():\n                counts = [tokenize_with_chat_template(tok, s) for s in samples]\n                stats = compute_stats(counts)\n                row = {\n                    \"condition\": condition,\n                    \"split\": split,\n                    \"model\": model_name,\n                    **stats,\n                }\n                all_results.append(row)\n                print(f\"  {condition}/{split}/{model_name}: mean={stats['mean']:.0f}, median={stats['median']:.0f}, max={stats['max']}, >2048={stats['pct_over_2048']:.1f}%\")\n    \n    # Print table\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*100)\n    print(f\"{'Condition':<18} {'Split':<7} {'Model':<22} {'Mean':>7} {'Median':>7} {'Max':>7} {'>2048%':>8} {'N':>6}\")\n    print(\"-\"*100)\n    for r in all_results:\n        print(f\"{r['condition']:<18} {r['split']:<7} {r['model']:<22} {r['mean']:>7.0f} {r['median']:>7.0f} {r['max']:>7} {r['pct_over_2048']:>7.1f}% {r['n']:>6}\")\n    print(\"=\"*100)\n    \n    # Save CSV\n    csv_path = OUT_DIR / \"tokenizer_stats.csv\"\n    with open(csv_path, \"w\", newline=\"\") as f:\n        writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=[\"condition\", \"split\", \"model\", \"mean\", \"median\", \"max\", \"pct_over_2048\", \"n\"])\n        writer.writeheader()\n        writer.writerows(all_results)\n    print(f\"\\nCSV saved to {csv_path}\")\n    \n    # Save JSON\n    json_path = OUT_DIR / \"tokenizer_stats.json\"\n    with open(json_path, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(all_results, f, indent=2)\n    print(f\"JSON saved to {json_path}\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nPYEOF\necho \"Script written.\"",

\"/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/hub/deepseek-coder-6.7b-instruct\",\n}\n\ndef load_samples(condition, split):\n    path = DATA_DIR / f\"{condition}_{split}.jsonl\"\n    samples = []\n    with open(path) as f:\n        for line in f:\n            samples.append(json.loads(line))\n    return samples\n\ndef tokenize_with_chat_template(tokenizer, sample):\n    messages = [\n        {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": sample[\"input\"]},\n        {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": sample[\"output\"]},\n    ]\n    text = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(messages, tokenize=False)\n    tokens = tokenizer.encode(text)\n    return len(tokens)\n\ndef compute_stats(token_counts, ctx_limit=2048):\n    arr = np.array(token_counts)\n    return {\n        \"mean\": round(float(np.mean(arr)), 1),\n        \"median\": round(float(np.median(arr)), 1),\n        \"max\": int(np.max(arr)),\n        \"pct_over_2048\": round(float(np.mean(arr > ctx_limit) * 100), 2),\n        \"n\": len(arr),\n    }\n\ndef main():\n    print(\"Loading tokenizers (CPU only, offline)...\")\n    tokenizers = {}\n    for name, path in MODELS.items():\n        print(f\"  Loading {name} from {path}...\")\n        tokenizers[name] = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(path, trust_remote_code=True)\n    print(\"Tokenizers loaded.\\n\")\n\n    all_results = []\n\n    for condition in CONDITIONS:\n        for split in SPLITS:\n            samples = load_samples(condition, split)\n            for model_name, tok in tokenizers.items():\n                counts = [tokenize_with_chat_template(tok, s) for s in samples]\n                stats = compute_stats(counts)\n                row = {\n                    \"condition\": condition,\n                    \"split\": split,\n                    \"model\": model_name,\n                    **stats,\n                }\n                all_results.append(row)\n                print(f\"  {condition}/{split}/{model_name}: mean={stats['mean']:.0f}, median={stats['median']:.0f}, max={stats['max']}, >2048={stats['pct_over_2048']:.1f}%\")\n\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*100)\n    print(f\"{'Condition':<18} {'Split':<7} {'Model':<22} {'Mean':>7} {'Median':>7} {'Max':>7} {'>2048%':>8} {'N':>6}\")\n    print(\"-\"*100)\n    for r in all_results:\n        print(f\"{r['condition']:<18} {r['split']:<7} {r['model']:<22} {r['mean']:>7.0f} {r['median']:>7.0f} {r['max']:>7} {r['pct_over_2048']:>7.1f}% {r['n']:>6}\")\n    print(\"=\"*100)\n\n    csv_path = OUT_DIR / \"tokenizer_stats.csv\"\n    with open(csv_path, \"w\", newline=\"\") as f:\n        writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=[\"condition\", \"split\", \"model\", \"mean\", \"median\", \"max\", \"pct_over_2048\", \"n\"])\n        writer.writeheader()\n        writer.writerows(all_results)\n    print(f\"\\nCSV saved to {csv_path}\")\n\n    json_path = OUT_DIR / \"tokenizer_stats.json\"\n    with open(json_path, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(all_results, f, indent=2)\n    print(f\"JSON saved to {json_path}\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nPYEOF\necho \"Script updated.\"",
  "account": "root"
}

### Assistant

_source line 106 · 2026-05-16T01:14:53.061Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The Qwen tokenizer uses `Qwen2Tokenizer` which is a standard transformers tokenizer class - no remote code needed. The issue is likely the `config.json` referencing the model, and the error comes from trying to load config to determine if there's a custom model class. Let me try without `trust_remote_code=True` and also add `local_files_only=True`.

Actually, looking at the error more carefully, the log shows that even with `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1`, it's still trying to reach HuggingFace. The null bytes in the log are suspicious - might be a binary output. 

I'll try forcing the model to load only from local files by setting `local_files_only=True` in the `from_pretrained` call.
```

</details>

### Assistant

_source line 107 · 2026-05-16T01:14:53.509Z_

Qwen2Tokenizer 是标准类，不需要 `trust_remote_code`。问题在于 transformers 仍在尝试联网验证。改用 `local_files_only=True` 并去掉 `trust_remote_code`。

Path(\"/root/autodl-tmp/trace-verdict-study-data\")\nOUT_DIR = Path(\"/root/trace-verdict-study/analysis\")\n\nCONDITIONS = [\"vota\", \"no_trace\", \"exception_only\", \"full_trace\", \"ae_only\", \"loop_only\"]\nSPLITS = [\"train\", \"test\"]\n\nMODELS = {\n    \"Qwen2.5-Coder-7B\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/trace-verdict-study-models/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct\",\n    \"DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/hub/deepseek-coder-6.7b-instruct\",\n}\n\ndef load_samples(condition, split):\n    path = DATA_DIR / f\"{condition}_{split}.jsonl\"\n    samples = []\n    with open(path) as f:\n        for line in f:\n            samples.append(json.loads(line))\n    return samples\n\ndef tokenize_with_chat_template(tokenizer, sample):\n    messages = [\n        {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": sample[\"input\"]},\n        {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": sample[\"output\"]},\n    ]\n    text = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(messages, tokenize=False)\n    tokens = tokenizer.encode(text)\n    return len(tokens)\n\ndef compute_stats(token_counts, ctx_limit=2048):\n    arr = np.array(token_counts)\n    return {\n        \"mean\": round(float(np.mean(arr)), 1),\n        \"median\": round(float(np.median(arr)), 1),\n        \"max\": int(np.max(arr)),\n        \"pct_over_2048\": round(float(np.mean(arr > ctx_limit) * 100), 2),\n        \"n\": len(arr),\n    }\n\ndef main():\n    print(\"Loading tokenizers (CPU only, local_files_only)...\", flush=True)\n    tokenizers = {}\n    for name, path in MODELS.items():\n        print(f\"  Loading {name} from {path}...\", flush=True)\n        tokenizers[name] = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(path, local_files_only=True)\n    print(\"Tokenizers loaded.\\n\", flush=True)\n\n    all_results = []\n\n    for condition in CONDITIONS:\n        for split in SPLITS:\n            samples = load_samples(condition, split)\n            for model_name, tok in tokenizers.items():\n                counts = [tokenize_with_chat_template(tok, s) for s in samples]\n                stats = compute_stats(counts)\n                row = {\n                    \"condition\": condition,\n                    \"split\": split,\n                    \"model\": model_name,\n                    **stats,\n                }\n                all_results.append(row)\n                print(f\"  {condition}/{split}/{model_name}: mean={stats['mean']:.0f}, median={stats['median']:.0f}, max={stats['max']}, >2048={stats['pct_over_2048']:.1f}%\", flush=True)\n\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*100, flush=True)\n    print(f\"{'Condition':<18} {'Split':<7} {'Model':<22} {'Mean':>7} {'Median':>7} {'Max':>7} {'>2048%':>8} {'N':>6}\", flush=True)\n    print(\"-\"*100, flush=True)\n    for r in all_results:\n        print(f\"{r['condition']:<18} {r['split']:<7} {r['model']:<22} {r['mean']:>7.0f} {r['median']:>7.0f} {r['max']:>7} {r['pct_over_2048']:>7.1f}% {r['n']:>6}\", flush=True)\n    print(\"=\"*100, flush=True)\n\n    csv_path = OUT_DIR / \"tokenizer_stats.csv\"\n    with open(csv_path, \"w\", newline=\"\") as f:\n        writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=[\"condition\", \"split\", \"model\", \"mean\", \"median\", \"max\", \"pct_over_2048\", \"n\"])\n        writer.writeheader()\n        writer.writerows(all_results)\n    print(f\"\\nCSV saved to {csv_path}\", flush=True)\n\n    json_path = OUT_DIR / \"tokenizer_stats.json\"\n    with open(json_path, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(all_results, f, indent=2)\n    print(f\"JSON saved to {json_path}\", _source line 116 · 2026-05-16T01:15:58.352Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Even with `local_files_only=True`, it's failing because the transformers library tries to load the config and can't find it in cache. The issue is that the model files are not in the standard HuggingFace cache format (with the `snapshots` directory), they're in a flat directory structure downloaded from ModelScope.

For the Qwen model, the files are directly in `/root/autodl-tmp/trace-verdict-study-models/Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct/`. For the DeepSeek model, they're at `/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/hub/deepseek-coder-6.7b-instruct/`.


The error message indicates it's trying to fetch from HuggingFace Hub even though I specified `local_files_only=True`, which suggests the tokenizer path might be incomplete or the DeepSeek model files aren't fully present locally. Looking at the traceback more carefully, the Qwen tokenizer actually loaded successfully, so the issue is specific to how the DeepSeek model is being referenced.

Let me check the transformers library version and verify that the DeepSeek tokenizer directory contains all the necessary files it needs.
```
